What Dragons Need

by Io

Character Select

Choose what race you wish to play as.

Human: Nobody really knows humanity's relation to the other mortals. The progenitors? An offshoot of one of the others? Sharing an ancestor? The answer is buried by history. Regardless, over the years humanity has shown its worth time and time again, and while they're nothing special in ability and temperament, they have come farther than would be expected for a race who only began recording history 8,000 years ago. Humans gain a minor bonus to experience gain.

Elf: While elves no longer have the ancient and vast empires that brought them glory, they are still a widely spread people covering all corners of the world. While it's no longer in practice today, a past history of internal eugenics means that the elves tend to be more magically inclined than their fellow races, for better or worse. Elves gain a minor magic bonus.

Orc: In ancient times, orcs formed many small tribes, barbarous and cruel, known for their ferocious strength and skill in battle. Those days are past now, and they live - mostly - in peace with the other races of the realm. While history is sprinkled with anti-orc sentiment, those days too are gone, and the orcish people live every day striving to show they are not their ancestors. Orcs gain a minor strength bonus.

Enter 'H' to select a human.

Enter 'E' to select an elf.

Enter 'O' to select an orc.

WARNING! THIS DECISION IS PERMANENT! BE VERY CERTAIN OF YOUR CHOICE!
> H

Stats Select

Your name is Kai Trinnal!

You have 6 stat points to spend!

Stamina: Each point of Stamina increases your Maximum Health. You currently have 10 Stamina.

Intellect: Each point of Intellect increases your Maximum Mana. You currently have 10 Intellect.

Constitution: Each point of Constitution reduces physical damage taken. You currently have 10 Constitution.

Willpower: Each point of Willpower reduces magical damage taken. You currently have 10 Willpower.

Second Floor
You can see your Bed, a Nightstand and a Closet.
You can go down.

This is your bedroom. People always say that it's not much, but you can't see what they mean. The brown wood is cozy. It's got a bed, a nightstand, and a closet holding your clothes. What more could you want? The door swings open to reveal a set of wooden stairs that go downstairs. Honestly, it's perfect.

Enter 'S' to view your stats.
> x bed

It's your bed! A little on the small side for a Human such as yourself, but it's yours and that's what matters. The frame is made of a pale wood you can never remember the name of, there is hay covering, and a white, feathery pillow. Currently the brown hand-woven sheet your mother gave you as a child is crumpled up in a ball. Making the bed is for the weak.
> go down
First Floor
In your house is a Dining Table and a Cupboard.
You can go up or north.

The first floor of your house is a little more elaborate than the second floor, but not by much. There's a dining table, but currently you've got all the seats pushed in. Cupboards line the walls. Unlit candles stand on the table, unneeded since brilliant sunlight is streaming in through your windows, casting everything in toasty yellow warmth. You can go up the stairs to your bedroom, or go out into town.

You've probably got work to do outside anyway.
> x cupboard

Dusty cupboards made of dark brown wood. They can open and close, but the hinges are a bit rusty.
> open cupboard
You open the cupboard. Maybe there's something inside?
Inside the cupboard you find an Apple.
> take apple
You grab the apple.
> eat apple

You eat the apple. Delicious! You're ready to face the day.
> go north
Streets of Tut'rial
You can go south, east or west.

You step outside your house and take a deep breath of the humid daytime air. The town of Tut'rial isn't very active on the best of days, and today is no exception. Still, there are a few crowds milling about.

In the center of town is a block of buildings, and around it is the main street. This results in a sort of 'ring' that people can traverse around.

From here, you can see your neighbors. To the west, someone is painting someone else's house. To the east is your workplace, the local smithy.
> go east
Streets of Tut'rial
You can go west, north or southeast.

The town of Tut'rial isn't very active on the best of days, and today is no exception. Still, there are a few crowds milling about.

In the center of town is a block of buildings, and around it is the main street. This results in a sort of 'ring' that people can traverse around.

To the west is your house, the door currently locked. To the north is the local eye doctor's residence. A more general practice doctor can be found way out in the fields, but she's not as specialized. From here, you can see your workplace, which you proudly call 'Ironing Out the Details'.
> go southeast
Ironing Out the Details
You can go northwest.

Your workplace. It's just like you left it, with the forge quiet, the cooling table humid. All your tools are in their place, and your stock iron is safely stowed away lest some idiot kids poke their eyes out with them. The air is filled with the comforting smell of iron and sweat, and lanterns provide you all the light you'll need.

You've got a lot to do today. May as well get started.

In the center of town is a block of buildings, and around it is the main street. This results in a sort of 'ring' that people can traverse around.

Around you are residential buildings, complete with clothes drying on strings, dogs barking and playing with children, and adults sitting in chairs talking with each other.
> go west
Streets of Tut'rial
You can go east, west or north.

The town of Tut'rial isn't very active on the best of days, and today is no exception. Still, there are a few crowds milling about.

In the center of town is a block of buildings, and around it is the main street. This results in a sort of 'ring' that people can traverse around.

Just north of you is the gate through which you can leave Tut'rial. Around you are various inns and taverns, for the scant few people passing through. To the east is a block of residential houses, and to the west is the town library.
> go west
Streets of Tut'rial
You can go east, south or northwest.

You continue to walk along the streets, humming to yourself quietly. To the southeast is a block of buildings, blocking off all passage. The road turns here, but on the outside of the 'ring' you can see the town library. It's a tall building, two stories and an attic, though only the first two are open to the public. A sign hangs outside a window, saying 'Open 24/7'.

To the east is the gate that leads out of town, and to the south is your favorite restaurant.
> go north
Library's Lower Floor
Two books catch your eye, a Blue Tome and a Red Tome.
You can go up or southeast.

The library's lower floor smells of dust and of old books, which coincidentally smell like dust. Shelves line the walls, lit by chandeliers on the wooden ceiling. Behind a desk, an elven woman sits and flips through a book in boredom. As you walk around she glances at you occasionally, but beyond that remains part of the background.

There aren't just shelves and a bored worker, though. There are tables and chairs for people to sit, signs explaining how to take out a book, which books cannot be taken out and are there for in-library use only, and so on. The doors lead outside to the rest of Tut'rial, and a winding stairwell disappears into the ceiling.
> x blue tome

It's a thick blue book with the title 'Elementary Magic Principles' written on the front in golden font. Looks like a schoolbook of some kind.
> go up
Library Second Floor
Your attention is drawn to a Thin Yellow Book.
You can go down.

The library's upper floor smells of dust and of old books, which coincidentally smell like dust. Shelves line the walls, lit by chandeliers on the wooden ceiling. There are windows here, letting you look down upon the rest of the town.

There aren't just bookshelves around, though. There are tables and chairs for people to sit and glance out the windows, signs explaining how to take out a book, which books cannot be taken out and are there for in-library use only, and so on. A winding stairwell disappears into the floor.

> C
Another Day at Work

Well, no time like the present!

You slip into your safety equipment and open up shop. Work is slow, which is typical. It's not as though you work round the clock handling many small orders, rather you get a small number of requests, spend a lot of time working on those, and get a lot of money for it. You spend the morning finishing up a knife for Tialin, which he comes by to pick up, paying you in the process.

As time passes, Riekna comes by your shop. Riekna is a mountain of an orcish woman. She stands as tall as most elves, with ragged tufts of black hair and skin the color of freshly watered grass. Her tusks look sharp enough to use as knives, and her eyes squint at everything in what you'd assume, if you didn't know better, was disdain. In reality, she squints because she needs spectacles but refuses to get any.

"Hey there, Kai. Everything alright today?"

You shrug. "As well as could be. You need anything?" you ask.

"Actually, yes. I was wondering if you could make me a candleholder?" She slips a small piece of paper to you, showing off a structure. It looks like a tightly wound coil of steel, but the end lifts off and forms a pig tail for holding.

You consider it a few moments. "Alright, it should be seventy five gold. Work's been slow today, so I'll see if I can get it down by tonight. Come by around... hmm, does sunset work for you?"

The orc nods. "It does. Thank you, Kai." She shrugs. "Well, I'll let you work. See you at sunset."

Alright. Time to get to work.

Enter 'C' to continue.
> C
Ironing Out the Details
Here with you is a Forge, an Iron Rod, a Blacksmith Hammer and a Cooling Table.

Your workplace. It's just like you left it, with the forge quiet, the cooling table humid. All your tools are in their place, and your stock iron is safely stowed away lest some idiot kids poke their eyes out with them. The air is filled with the comforting smell of iron and sweat, and lanterns provide you all the light you'll need.

Time to make a candlestick holder. Shouldn't be too hard, you've got all the tools.

> take iron rod
You grab the iron rod. Time to get to work.
> use iron rod on forge

Ironing Out the Details
Here with you is a Forge, a Blacksmith Hammer and a Cooling Table.

Your workplace. It's just like you left it, with the forge quiet, the cooling table humid. All your tools are in their place, and your stock iron is safely stowed away lest some idiot kids poke their eyes out with them. The air is filled with the comforting smell of iron and sweat, and lanterns provide you all the light you'll need.

Time to make a candlestick holder. Shouldn't be too hard, you've got all the tools.

Enter 'Help' for some basic commands to use on objects, whether or not they outright say you can do them.

You put the iron rod into the forge. It heats up until it's glowing brilliantly, and malleable.
> take heated iron rod
With safety equipment, you pick up the heated rod.
> take blacksmith hammer
This is useful for your work.
> use blacksmith hammer on heated iron rod

You carefully use the hammer to bend the heated rod into shape. Once you're done, it's in a coil.
> take heated iron coil
With safety equipment, you pick up the heated coil.
> use heated iron coil on cooling table

You put the burning hot coil onto the table and wait for it to cool.

...

...

...

You test it with water. It's done; you've made a candleholder! Just in time too, Riekna's here.
> take candleholder
You pick up the candleholder.
> give candleholder to riekna

Riekna takes the candleholder and nods to you. "Thanks a million, Kai." She hands you her payment. "See you around!" she says over her shoulder as she walks out.

You gained 18 XP!

Enter 'S' to view your stats, including XP!

In the center of town is a block of buildings, and around it is the main street. This results in a sort of 'ring' that people can traverse around.

To the south is Ms. Havenshrine's clinic, and to the west the road leads towards the town gate, through which you can leave Tut'rial. Around you are residential buildings, complete with clothes drying on strings, dogs barking and playing with children, and adults sitting in chairs talking with each other.
> go west
Streets of Tut'rial
You can go east, west or north.

The town of Tut'rial isn't very active on the best of days, and today is no exception. Hoards of people seem to be milling up north, towards the gate that leads out of town. Wonder what's going on?

In the center of town is a block of buildings, and around it is the main street. This results in a sort of 'ring' that people can traverse around.

Just north of you is the gate through which you can leave Tut'rial. Around you are various inns and taverns, for the scant few people passing through. To the east is a block of residential houses, and to the west is the town library.
> go north
Iron Gate
He wasn't there before, but you can talk with the Recruitment Officer.
You can go south or north.

This is the gate that leads out of Tut'rial. It's giant, vaulted, and a work of art, framed with pictures of orcs, humans, and elves fighting against a horde of unsightly creatures.

The gate to the north leads to a wide open dirt road which stretches into the distance, but you can't think of anything you'd need out there right now.

There are enormous crowds of people here of all three races, pushing and shoving and shouting to be heard over each other. You push your way through the crowd to the front, where you find the center of commotion. A large military wagon has arrived through the gate, though calling it a 'wagon' might be a little unfair.

It's designed to travel from city to city, town to town, and pick up military recruits. A team of horses at the front stomp the ground in idle boredom, and the wagon's colossal body is covered with a sheet to serve as shelter from the elements, and the end is a closed wooden door. You can't see anyone inside, if there's anyone at all. Standing beside the wagon is a team of military recruitors, each with their own line of townsfolk. They gesture wildly, but whatever it is they're talking about it lost in the din of the crowd.

Maybe you should get in line and talk to one? The one on the left has the shortest line...
> speak to recruitment officer

You get in line and wait. After what feels like hours, you're talking with the recruitment officer.

"What's going on?" you ask. "I know you've probably heard this question a hundred times today but - "

"There's a war," the man explains. "Not going to lie, things are dire. Seven towns have already been destroyed. Just clean off the map." He makes a hand motion. "Poof."
> speak to recruitment officer

That doesn't sound good. Entire towns vanishing? "What could cause that?" you ask, wringing your hands nervously.

"Dragons," he says bluntly. You swear you feel the crowd hush for a moment. "Scouts confirmed it. Yes I know, I wore the same face." Realizing you're gawking, you shut your mouth. "I mean, creatures of myth and legend coming back and blowing up towns left and right for no discernable reason? Sounds fishy to me. But regardless of the cause, they're here and they're strong. We need all the soldiers we can get, or else... well, they certainly don't show any sign of stopping."

Something burns inside of you. If this man is the be believed, dragons are coming to wage war on the three races of the Union. Are you just going to take that laying down?
> speak to recruitment officer

"I think I'd like to enlist in that case," you say. "If it's as bad as you say - " Which it might not be, exageratting the danger just to get more soldiers, but that's not a rabbit hole you want to go down. " - then I need to at least try. Sign me up."

The man nods. "I'm glad to hear that, but it's not as simple as just hopping in that there wagon. You'll need to sign up some paperwork, and of course make sure your affairs are all in order. Remember, these are legendary dragons we're talking about. Go get things set, and talk to me again when you're ready."
> speak to recruitment officer

You take a deep breath. "I'm ready."

The man nods. "Alrighty then, let's get started."

Enter 'C' to continue.
> C
Getting Ready for War

Right. Can't go to war unprepared. You rush home and lock yourself in, working through your belongings in a flurry. Last will and testament, just in case. Clothes, though you don't know if you'll be needing them. Some food for the trip... there's honestly not a lot to pack but you pack whatever you think will be useful.

Once that's done you sprint back to the army wagon. You fill out a few sheets of paper provided to you, signing your name 'there, there, and there' as instructed once you've read. Everything seems agreeable.

With that done, you walk into the army wagon and let the door shut behind you. You're off to fight the dragons.

Enter 'C' to continue.

Wagon
In the wagon with you is Orc, a Haybale, Human and Elf.

You are riding in the army wagon. The windows are covered, so while some light streams in there's not much scenery. The ground is carpeted with hay, and in the corner are rancid smelling buckets covered in tarps. You're not alone in sitting on a haybale; currently three others are in the wagon with you, leaning against the wall and daydreaming.

The wagon bounces and rolls as it heads out to its destination. You can hear the horses trotting along and the wheels bouncing over boulders in the dirt roads.

Wagon

The orc turns to you and grins, baring his canines. "Hey hey! Lookie here!" He reaches out and clasps your hand with both of his. "Nice to meet you! Name's Arlaka. You?" His voice is baritone and jubulent, like holly-jolly Grandfather Winter.

"Kai, pleased to meet you," you respond.

"Pleasure's all mine! I think I'll like going through training with you! You've got some real muscle on you, too!"

"Uh, sure," you say.

Wagon
In the wagon with you is Arlaka, a Haybale, Human and Elf.

You are riding in the army wagon. The windows are covered, so while some light streams in there's not much scenery. The ground is carpeted with hay, and in the corner are rancid smelling buckets covered in tarps. You're not alone in sitting on a haybale; currently three others are in the wagon with you, leaning against the wall and daydreaming.

The wagon bounces and rolls as it heads out to its destination. You can hear the horses trotting along and the wheels bouncing over boulders in the dirt roads.

He cracks open an eye at your intrusion. "Hmm?"

"Apologies. I just wanted to make some small talk. Know anything about the dragons?" you ask, gesturing politely to him.

He shrugs. "Worked as a historian a few years ago. Really not a lot known about back then. So, you know the whole demon invasion thing, right? At Zero A.I.? Records show they were around then, but afterwards they turned on the mortal races for an unknown reason." He cracks a grin. "Course, the lizards were still recovering from the demons, so our wizards back then had no problem locking them in caves."

"Thanks for the info," you say. But he's already back to sleep.

Wagon
In the wagon with you is Arlaka, a Haybale, Human and Elf.

You are riding in the army wagon. The windows are covered, so while some light streams in there's not much scenery. The ground is carpeted with hay, and in the corner are rancid smelling buckets covered in tarps. You're not alone in sitting on a haybale; currently three others are in the wagon with you, leaning against the wall and daydreaming.

The wagon bounces and rolls as it heads out to its destination. You can hear the horses trotting along and the wheels bouncing over boulders in the dirt roads.

"It's curious," the elven woman says as you approach her. "The dragons were rumored to be highly intelligent, yet they're acting more like a destructive force of nature than a warring nation. No demands. No threats. Just mindless destruction."

"Intelligent?" you ask as the wagon jolts underfoot. "Aren't they just animals?"

The elf shakes her head. "Maybe they were descended from 'just animals' but... if the legends are correct and these truly are the dragons of myth, do not ever mistake them as being mere beasts. They can think and plan, and likely feel the full range of emotions like we do. Not at all like beasts, or even magical constructs. And sadly, unlike those we can't just hunt them or find their shutdown spell."

Enter 'C' to continue.
Training

After many days and nights, during which you pick up recruits until the interior of the wagon is packed, you finally arrive at your barracks where you'll be quickly whipped into semi-decent fighting shape and sent after the dragons. It's... not that impressive. To be specific, it's a tiny shack in the middle of a field of golden wheat.

It must be an underground complex, then.

Your wagon opens and then the shouting begins, telling you to get out, get moving, get in the shack and get down. You dutifully obey, sprinting alongside the others into the shack where, sure enough, there are stairs leading into the depths of the barracks.

What follows next is weeks of getting into shape. Laps. Pull ups. Push ups. Weights. Fighting. Endurance. Magic. Over and over and over. Thanks to your profession you've never been in particularly poor shape, but by the heavens this is ridiculous! But worse than the training itself is the knowledge that you're not actually doing anything. The dragon war is still going on, and if the giant conference hall's map is any indication, they're spreading out to wash over the continent at an alarming rate.

Sure, victories are being won. Their weaknesses are being found. But it's not looking good and you need to be out there, doing something about it!

But there's nothing you can do. You just have to suck it up and go with it. Eventually, you'll get to help. In the meantime, you go to the sparring ring and look for a partner.

Interestingly enough, it's a partner that finds you.

"Hey there!" she chirps. It's a human woman, dressed in full plate. You can't see much of her beneath the armor, but her skin is light like someone who spends all their time out of the sun. She's wielding a long two-handed sword, and behind her eyeslits two gray eyes stare back at you. "Saw you looking around." She walks forward, holding the sword effortlessly in one hand, and extends the other arm. You take the offered hand and shake it. "Name's Elizabeth. Elizabeth Gray."

"Kai," you say in response.

"Charmed to meet you," she says, a hidden smile evident in her tone. "So, how about it? Let's go a round?"

You take out your own sword, a mirror match of Elizabeth's. "Sure thing," you say.

"Great!" She takes a stance. "Here goes!"

Appearance
You can go east.

Your name is Kai Trinnal.

You are a Human man in your mid thirties, and you feel you've done fairly well for your age. Your body is stocky and strong, with muscles built up from long years working as your village's blacksmith. You stand at a solid 165 centimeters, when you aren't wearing shoes.

Your hair is brown, like hot cocoa, and is currently cut short. Your eyes aren't your most remarkable feature, being just a steady blue, and your ears are average shells as is normal for your race. Your smile is wide and only slightly crooked.

Your skin is a light tannish brown, scorched by the sun.

Appearance
You can go east.

Your name is Kai Trinnal.

You are a Human man in your mid thirties, and you feel you've done fairly well for your age. Your body is stocky and strong, with muscles built up from long years working as your village's blacksmith. You stand at a solid 165 centimeters, when you aren't wearing shoes.

Your hair is brown, like hot cocoa, and is currently cut short. Your eyes aren't your most remarkable feature, being just a steady blue, and your ears are average shells as is normal for your race. Your smile is wide and only slightly crooked.

Your skin is a light tannish brown, scorched by the sun.

On your head, you are wearing an Armored Helmet.

On your chest, you are wearing an Armored Chestplate.

On your legs, you are wearing Armored Leggings.

On your feet, you are wearing Armored Boots.

The time is 21:10
> go north
Dragon Attack!

It's time.

It's time for you to save the world... well, to help save the world.

All of you march out of the barracks and line up in the field. Warriors in front, battlemages right behind, mages and archers and many more taking up formation until you resemble a massive arrowhead in the fields of wheat. Elizabeth catches your eye and she winks, mouthing 'Relax!'. You take her advice and inhale deeply.

"Listen up!" an orcish man shouts at the front, dressed in full plate with the insignia of two doves crossing their beaks on his breastplate. "Enemy forces have been spotted via scryers headed to the town of Engim. And yes, it's dragons. At least a half dozen of them, and unidentified ground forces about our size. Engim's on the way to the city Bolvek, so it's important to hold this town and ensure they aren't surrounded." Bolvek. You remember that city. You'd actually been there once, long ago, on business. Nice city, great seafood. "We're heading by wagon," he says, gesturing to the series of packed wagons rolling up, pulled by teams of horses. "Teams are as follows! First wagon is Elizabeth, Taren..."

The orc continues to rattle off names, placing you in the third wagon. Tingling with anticipation, you get on when it arrives, amidst the clanking of clinking of everyone else rushing in. The few windows there are are opened up to let the spring breeze in as you start, the horses clip-clopping their way from your little 'shed' in the woods.

The trip takes a day, during which you take your time to steady yourself. Fear tingles in your gut. You could die out there. There's a very good chance you will die out there. But you're in too deep now, and this is what you decided to do.

Three days and two nights pass. On the third night, Engim appears on the horizon... and it's burning.

"Hold up!" you hear someone shout from outside the wagon. There's the sound of whips and horses slowing down. The bouncing wagon slows down, and the door's busted open. Everyone with you charges out, as do you. The moment you're outside, the smell of horse, armor and sweat is blasted away, replaced with the chill of night and the smell of smoke.

Engim's close. It's a small town, not much bigger than Tut'rial. From it, small fires glow brightly in the night, the inferno blotting out the stars with its light. People are running all about, carrying buckets of water. Above, an enormous shadow passes. The shadow approaches a building, and you get a good look at it.

A dragon.

It stands on four powerful legs, and is covered entirely in deep indigo scales like armor. Its paws each end in three sharp, curved talons, and under its scaly hide enormously powerful muscles shift and ripple. Its neck is long, like a swan's, and ends in an angular, snouted head as large as, well, you. Its blue eyes are slitted, and from its skull sprout two thick, ivory horns. From the dragon's body extend two enormous bat wings, the bones capped with black talons. A muscular tail doubles the length of the dragon, complete with a spade of bone on the very end. Its size is such that just the head is as tall as you are.

"Holy shit," you whisper. The dragon alights on a nearby building and takes a deep breath. You're prepared for a blast of fire, but instead a river of icicles large enough to tear a man in two shoot from its mouth and into the invisible street below. It flaps its wings furiously and takes off again. Around the town, more magical blasts of various types - fire among them - rain from the shadows above.

You storm into the town, ready to defend it, but fear settles in your gut.

How are you possibly going to defend the town from THAT?

Enter 'C' to continue.

You're standing on the streets of Engim. Cobble roads are beneath your feet, and houses in various stages of destruction surround you. Rubble spills onto the streets. From here you can go either west or north. To the east are the local historical records, and a stairwell up leads to a roof where you might be able to strike a blow against these dragons.

Shadows cross over through the night sky, and every now and then the gloom is pierced with either fire, air, ice or earth magic from above.

Engim Cathedral
You can see a Pinch of Gunpowder.
You can go southwest.

Engim's cathedral is a stone white building that isn't particularly tall. Its white bricks are sturdy, but the stained glass windows are shattered from dragon bombardment. Tucked away, out of sight from the windows where a dragon could lean its head in and unleash its wrath, are multiple cots onto which the wounded have been moved for treatment.

You can hear the thud thud of the ongoing dragon bombardment outside. You're safe in here for the time being, but there's probably not much you can do from in here.

Appearance
You can go east.

Your name is Kai Trinnal.

You are a Human man in your mid thirties, and you feel you've done fairly well for your age. Your body is stocky and strong, with muscles built up from long years working as your village's blacksmith. You stand at a solid 165 centimeters, when you aren't wearing shoes.

Your hair is brown, like hot cocoa, and is currently cut short. Your eyes aren't your most remarkable feature, being just a steady blue, and your ears are average shells as is normal for your race. Your smile is wide and only slightly crooked.

Your skin is a light tannish brown, scorched by the sun.

On your head, you are wearing an Armored Helmet.

On your chest, you are wearing a Damaged Wyrmblood Chestplate.

On your legs, you are wearing Armored Leggings.

On your feet, you are wearing Armored Boots.

Your hands grip an enchanted sword..

Shadows cross over through the night sky, and every now and then the gloom is pierced with either fire, air, ice or earth magic from above.
> go up
Defensive Roof
Mounted to the rooftop is a Harpoon Gun.
You can go down.

You stand atop a raised platform, marred by flame and ice. Next to you is a harpoon gun.

Shadows cross over through the night sky, and every now and then the gloom is pierced with either fire, air, ice or earth magic from above. This gun must be good for SOMETHING!
> repair harpoon gun

You get to work, trying to ignore the enormous dragons swooping around you, raining magical blasts on the town. Before long the harpoon gun is... operational. It only has one shot anyway.
> fire harpoon gun

You spot a dragon flying too close. It's unaware of the danger in this small, formerly broken device. You pull the trigger.

KACHUNK!

The dragon plummets to the ground, flightless, and boy does it look pissed!
Battle
You can check your Enemy and Yourself.
You can try to flee by going out.

The indigo dragon you shot down looks pissed beyond belief! Luckily, you're not alone in this fight. Many other soldiers are here to assist you in bringing down this beast!

A Clash Occurs

You stay back, watching your opponent intently.

A squadron of mages stands back and unleashes a volley of magic. Fireballs, arcane blasts, and stone spikes crash upon the injured dragon.

35 instances of 3 magical damage!

Soldiers engage the dragon in melee, trying to crawl atop it, stabbing and slicing at any scales they can find.

30 instances of 3 physical damage!

Archers on a nearby rooftop unleash their arrows. They fall in a single salvo of darkness upon the dragon, puncturing between the scales.

40 instances of 3 physical damage!

The dragon lashes its tail behind it, where you currently are! Thanks to having waited, you can duck beneath the muscular length, but a few soldiers behind you are not so lucky. The boney spade on the end of the tail smashes them aside like flies!

Appearance
You can go east.

Your name is Kai Trinnal.

You are a Human man in your mid thirties, and you feel you've done fairly well for your age. Your body is stocky and strong, with muscles built up from long years working as your village's blacksmith. You stand at a solid 165 centimeters, when you aren't wearing shoes.

Your hair is brown, like hot cocoa, and is currently cut short. Your eyes aren't your most remarkable feature, being just a steady blue, and your ears are average shells as is normal for your race. Your smile is wide and only slightly crooked.

Your skin is a light tannish brown, scorched by the sun.

On your head, you are wearing an Armored Helmet.

On your chest, you are wearing a Damaged Wyrmblood Chestplate.

On your legs, you are wearing Armored Leggings.

On your feet, you are wearing Armored Boots.

Your hands grip an enchanted sword..

Enter 'B' to return.
> go east

The fight goes on well into the night. More ammunition is brought out, and the harpoons ground more dragons. Engim Historical Records
Here with you is the Museum Curator.
You can go west.

Engim's historical records isn't quite a museum, but you'd guess it's the closest such a small town can muster. Stacks upon stacks of scrolls are piled on golden tables, organized by some method you have no hope of understanding. There's a sizable hole in one of the walls, but other than that the red-brick building is entirely intact.

With the danger gone, you can actually see the curator here at the front desk, looking through records with bemused 'hmm's.
> speak to museum curator

"Hello," you say to the old man.

"Hmm?" he grunts, glancing up from his work. "Ah, the local hero. What can I do for you?" he asks, sitting up.

"I was just wondering, if these are the historical archives, there must be something in here about the dragons and where they came from?" you ask.

He nods. "Ah yes, I was just researching that myself," he says, gesturing to his work. "So you know about the demon war. Ancient history. It was a big war, but all things considered not worthy of basing our entire system of time around. What comes after though, is the dragons turning on mortal kind. Records show they utilized dark magic for nefarious purposes. They created magical constructs called Corinaders out of that foul power. They were strong and multiplied like rabbits. The Corinaders washed over the world, killing any mortals they could find with the aid of the dragons. Luckily, though, the dragons took the worst from the demon war, so we were - at great cost, mind you - able to beat back the Corinaders and our magicians sealed the dragons deep beneath the land."

He taps his chin. "I actually think you can see the mountain they were buried underneath from here, Mt. Vulcis. You'd have to climb one of the trees in the woods though, so I don't recommend it."
> go west

> climb trees

Maybe you can see the mountain from here?

You brace yourself and grab a branch. Then another. Then another. But then...

CRACK!

The branch you're clinging to snaps and you fall all the way down. Luckily there's a strange bed of moss to cushion your fall...
> tear bed of moss

You grab onto the moss and start ripping it up. The stone beneath seems to give way as well, until soon you realize this is a cleverly concealed entrance to a cave!
> go down
Wet Cavern
You can see a Mystic Cowl.
You can go up.

A damp cave, hidden beneath a moss covering. Huh. Looks like some wizard wanted to be fancy about hiding their stuff.
> take mystic cowl
> equip mystic cowl
> go up
A Dragon Appears!

As you climb up from the cave, satisfied but still trying to see the mountain, a shadow flies overhead. You duck back down into the cave, only your head sticking out, and watch.

CRASH!

A colossal dragon slams down into the forest, its sheer weight flattening the trees and forcibly clearing a space for it. The dragon's scales are golden yellow and shimmer in the sunlight, casting rays of light in all directions. Its head is proud and the horns are long and noble. It swings its gaze around, inhaling deeply through its nostrils.

Terror roots itself in your legs like ice, paralyzing you. You duck lower. It can't see you. It can't see you, it can't see you...

"I can see you there, mortal," a masculine voice says in perfect Common. There's a bit of a growling accent, but it's definitely Common. And the words themselves only serve to make your muscles lock up tighter. "I bear you no ill will, please. Won't you come out?"

Well to hell with it. It already knows you're here. Unless it's bluffing, but you doubt that. Shaking, you rise to your feet and climb out of the shallow cave and stand before the male dragon.

It's even bigger up close.

"Ah, there you are," he muses. "Allow me to introduce myself. I am Corona."

"... Kai," you say warily.

"A splendid name! Now." He raises a foreleg in a comforting gesture. "I understand you do not currently have the best impression of my kind, but I am here to make you an offer. Despite the recent... " He seems to mull over for the word. "... mistake we made with this town here, we are making excellent progress. However, know that we are not bent on utterly exterminating your kind down to the last, young Human. To some, we will offer mercy."

The gears in your head turn. "That person in the forge, he attacked me. Mortals on your side?"

"People join the Wyrmblood for a variety of reasons. In return for a promise of clemency and great power, they in return are made incapable of bearing children and are pledged to our service. An offer I would extend to you, young one. I promise you, your chances of survival with us are far greater than against us." Corona extends the paw he raised earlier. "Will you?"

Your head whirls. Join the dragons. The monsters razing the countryside and massacring your people. As if! You'll fight to your last breath. You raise your weapon in defiance.

Corona sighs. "Well, I cannot say I am surprised. I do think you are operating off incomplete information though. If you'll follow me... ?"

You stay rooted.

The dragon rolls his slitted red eyes. "Oh very well. The hard way it is, then."

> C
The Wyrmblood

At some point during your abduction, you must've nodded off to the tune of nauseating flight and blood loss. When you come back around, you're laying in a cot and someone's forcing your head up to drink water. Your vision's blurry, but you think you can make out the visage of a human woman offering you the water. Then you're back asleep.

You wake up a few more times, and go back to sleep just as quickly. Eventually though, you come back around and stay that way.

You look around. You're in what looks like an infirmary ward. Cots - mostly unoccupied - surround you, and the room you're in is absolutely enormous. Enough to hold a small town. You're bandaged up and out of your gear, which you see laying on a pile nearby. There's also something tremendously large in your vision.

You look at it and wait until your eyes aren't blurring anymore. Then Corona's draconic visage comes into view, and you groan.

"I'm sorry it had to be this way," the dragon explains. "But you were being difficult. And while our... task is rather bloody, there's no need for people to die if it can be helped." Corona rolls his eyes. "That's my view on it anyway. Anyway, welcome to one of our Wyrmblood camps. I'll be around if you wish to speak, and feel free to speak to the others. Get a feel for our side of the war."

"Your side?" you sputter. "You attacked us completely unprovoked."

Corona turns his head sideways and raises a scaly brow. "Feel free to grab your gear, you're no danger here." That's just insulting. "Do understand you are technically our prisoner. Lithoa's guarding the exit, and you won't get past her. Brown dragon, understand. She'll be able to feel your footsteps if you try to get past her. Beyond that, enjoy. If you feel persuaded, then at the very center of the camp is Captain Bijorn. Talk to him to join our ranks, mortal."

"And if I don't?" you ask.

Corona shrugs his colossal wings up and down. "Then you stay here until all the rest of your kind are... dealt with. And then you'll probably die. Those are your options," he says, turning around and squeezing out of the exit, tail lashing behind him.

Join the dragons or die. There has to be a way out, though. But you can't lie, joining the dragons probably WOULD guarantee your survival.

Elizabeth and Arlaka flash through your mind. They'd hate you... but maybe you could get them to join as well.

You shake your head. No sense focusing on that yet. You put your gear on and head out. You can join the Wyrmblood, or look for a way out. Time to go.

> join the wyrmblood general bijorn
Wyrmblood Initiation

"I've thought it over," you say at last, a lump forming in your throat. You think of Arlaka and Elizabeth. What your deceased parents would think of you. You're turning your back on all of them. But at the same time...

"I want to join the Wyrmblood," you say haltingly.

General Bijorn stares at you. "You understand the ramifications of this, yes? You'll be turning your back on the Union, officially labeling yourself as an enemy. The dragons will be your superiors from now until the day you die."

"I know, and I understand," you say. "I still want to." But in your gut, you know this is cowardly treason and there's no way around it. Somehow, somewhen, karma will punish you for this. You're sure of it.

The human man smiles. "Then let's get the ritual started."
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
You expected some lavish ceremony, with monks in dark robes circling around you and chanting. Instead you're standing right at the edge of the camp, with the forests closing in around you. Only three people are attendance. There's General Bijorn, holding a thin book in front of him. There's Corona, the golden dragon that brought you here. And Lithoa, the earthy brown dragoness guarding the camp's exit.

"This is a newly written oath, so you'll have to bear with me," the general says. "Repeat after me," he says, flipping open to another page. "I, Kai."

"I, Kai."

"So solemly swear to pledge my allegiance, now and forever, to Queen Aerona of the dragons, and her kind." You repeat the words, trembling near the end. "I burn my bridges and sever my ties to my old life. I shall serve the dragons faithfully, until the day it is decided I am no longer needed for their goals. So I swear." You finish repeating the vow, your stomach twisting around sickeningly.

General Bijorn nods solemly and closes the book. "There's only one step left, Kai. We are not known as the Wyrmblood for nothing." He reaches inside his clothing and pulls out holy shit that's a knife! "You may have noticed the great power we wield. It is granted to us in order to keep up with the dragons who command us. Their magicians enchant armor for us in the depths of Mt. Vulcis, but that is not the only source of our power. To complete the pact, you must have dragon blood inside of you."

Your eyes widen, but your legs feel as though they're stuck to the ground so you can't take a single step back. "Isn't that incredibly dangerous? Mixing different bloods together?"

Corona, who's been silent until now, shakes his head. "In this situation, no. The ambient magic of our blood will fuse itself to your soul, and prevent any of the medical hazards otherwise present for either of us. As there are only two of us here, your choice of blood is from either myself or Lithoa."

Choice? "I have a choice?"

Corona nods. "It will not influence much, dragon blood is equally powerful regardless of the dragons themselves, but it will provide you very minor, unconscious even, control over our aspects. Fire for myself." He gestures with a wing to Lithoa, who's been staring at you curiously. "And earth for Lithoa here. Make your choice, young one."

'L': Lithoa's Blood.

'C': Corona's Blood.
> C
Null
Wyrmblood Initiation

"Ah," Corona says, raising a foreleg to his scaly chest. "I am flattered. General, if you will?"

Bijorn nods and steps closer to you, brandishing the steak knife. You gulp, but hold out your left hand. The human grabs your hand with his free one and presses the knife against your arm, away from anything important. He looks you in the eyes. "Don't flinch," he warns.

You take a deep breath and, to distract yourself, look towards Coro - OW OW it hurts so much more than in battle for some reason!

Corona approaches you, shaking the earth with his steps. You crane your neck back, your world enveloped with yellow scales the size of your hand. Sitting on his haunches, he holds his left foreleg out and presses the claws of his other leg against it. For a moment he growls with effort, but then he makes a small incision and brilliant red blood, brighter than you'd noticed dragon blood is, beads against his armored hide. Without further ado, he pushes his cut into yours, and you take a deep breath.

Fire burns through your veins, and a hot flash drenches your skin with sweat. You stumble away from the other three, one hand around your cut and the other fanning air onto your face. Eventually the rush of heat ends, and Bijorn is in front of you. Even after the initial flash, you still feel... strong.

"Welcome to the Wyrmblood," he says.

> speak to general bijorn
Assault on Bovek

You gather in the church with quite a few other Wyrmblood. You came early so you manage to get a good seat near the middle, but soon they're crowding in, taking up every seat, and then sitting on the ground in a tide of enchanted armor.

"Thank you all for being here," General Bijorn says, standing up front next to a table. "Here's the situation. With the mortals entrenching, the dragons are finding that their casaulties are climbing to higher than expected. Given they're already extremely endangered," Endangered? The dragons are endangered? " - they've opted to summon the Corinaders. The Bane needs reagents before he can, though. We don't have any of the things we need to start Corinader production. The  city of Bovek, however, does!"

Whispers fill the room, and Bijorn raises his hand. "Quiet! Now, Corona has authorized pallets for us to ride in on, and he's brought a few other dragons to deliver them. Lithoa will remain here as guard, but Corona will be going with us. He will be your commanding officer on-site. We're expecting heavy resistance. Archers here, here and here..." he says, going over to his table and holding up the map.

General Bijorn elaborates on the plan for a bit, assigning certain people to certain parts of the city. You get to go downtown. A camp will be set up just outside the city's borders, for collecting and moving reagents.

"Understood?" Bijorn asks. "Get in, clear out the enemy's air defenses, get the Corinader reagants to Corona, get out. Now, dismissed!"

The room explodes into movement as the Wyrmblood on the ground struggle to get up. Everyone pushes and shoves, as do you, and soon you're outside, in the camp.

Bijorn wasn't lying. There were indeed other dragons here. Gray ones, indigoes, yellows, and browns too, standing around on all fours. There are pallets too, which are nothing more than giant wooden boxes, with walls that could be folded up and down, along with seats inside. Corona's currently speaking to the dragons, gesturing with his tail, forelegs, and wings.

There's a lot of coordination involved. You end up taking some rations, getting to the dragon who will be bringing you to downtown - an indigo wyrm, who introduces herself as Cryoniosa - and enter the pallet strapped to her back. You take a seat, others file in around you, and soon you're off.

The box lurches, and a few of the Wyrmblood around you stumble in their seats as you lift off. Your heart hammers in your chest. This is it. Your first mission as a Wyrmblood. Traitor to your people. You're going to help the dragons create the magical constructs known as Corinaders, which had nearly wiped out your people in ancient times.

You swallow, but focus. You've made your choice. Whatever brought you down this path, it's the one you have to stick on. After all, what are you going to do? Betray the dragons? The giant, flying, magic-breathing dragons?

Soon, you're descending. The pallet's walls open, and you all spill out into the streets of Bovek.

It's the middle of the night. Fires rage in the distance, and a particularly tall wall of fire blocks the way out of downtown. Corona, the yellow dragon that brought you to your new life, hovers not far away, beating his wings against the air and surveying the area.

It's time. Maybe you should go talk to him, get a better sense of what to do?

Corona is here, flapping his wings with a firewall at his back. You can probably ask him what to do next.

Your name is Kai Trinnal.

You are a Human man in your mid thirties, and you feel you've done fairly well for your age. Your body is stocky and strong, with muscles built up from long years working as your village's blacksmith. You stand at a solid 165 centimeters, when you aren't wearing shoes.

Your hair is brown, like hot cocoa, and is currently cut short. Your eyes aren't your most remarkable feature, being just a steady blue, and your ears are average shells as is normal for your race. Your smile is wide and only slightly crooked.

Your name is Kai Trinnal.

You are a Human man in your mid thirties, and you feel you've done fairly well for your age. Your body is stocky and strong, with muscles built up from long years working as your village's blacksmith. You stand at a solid 165 centimeters, when you aren't wearing shoes.

Your hair is brown, like hot cocoa, and is currently cut short. Your eyes aren't your most remarkable feature, being just a steady blue, and your ears are average shells as is normal for your race. Your smile is wide and only slightly crooked.

Your skin is a light tannish brown, scorched by the sun.

You can see a Holy Water.

Bovek's Cathedral is even more impressive from the inside. Golden light streams through the windows, casting ominous shadows. Statues of various saints throughout history line the edges, marble pillars rise to the heavens, and the ceiling is painted with a picture of the planet, with the single continent in the middle. Various gods float around it, imparting their powers to the world.

You look around a little, heeding Corona's advice. Enter 'C' to continue.
> C
The Dragonslayer

You walk through the burning streets of Bovek, searching for anything you may have overlooked. Some gear you could use, a few potions for the stockpile, or books with valuable knowledge for the long locked away dragons. There's not many people challenging you, and those that do are easy to... dispatch... with your Wyrmblood powers.

Still, you can't help your stomach churning. What do they think of you, in their dying moments? Another traitor? Some poor sap who's been had?

You climb past overturned wagons and collapsed buildings, searching. You're in a little bit of a hurry, after all. Don't want to be left behind.

Tap.

That's all the warning you get.

You spin around and jump backwards as a dull red sword swings at where your neck was just a moment ago, swishing through the air. You whirl around, weapon at the ready, and face your attacker.

It's hard to tell under the armor, but you think it's an orcish man. His sword is long, dull red and notched, covered with glowing orange runes that spell out a word you can't read from here. The hilt is jeweled, and even from where you are you can feel a magical breeze flowing off the sword. The man's armor is like nothing you've ever seen before. It's thick but seems to bend easily. It's brilliant orange, made of some enchanted steel, and stylized with feathery symbols and two bird wings on the helmet. Enchanted flame billows from the armor, coiling along the metal plates but leaving no smoke. It's hard to look at it due to the intensity of the flame, but there's no doubt about it.

You recognize that armor from books. All blacksmiths do. The legendary Phoenix Armor, said to give near immortality and great control over life magic. But where did he find it? And who's wearing it?

"... Kai?" the man whispers in shock. Your stomach drops. It's Arlaka. "Please, tell me it isn't so," he says.

You gulp, but nod. "It is. I'm sorry Arlaka."

"Sorry doesn't cut it!" the armored orc shouts suddenly. Terrified, you take a step back. "You're assisting genocide! The aggressors! You threw your lot in with them! What, because you were scared? Look at me! You recognize this armor. See this sword?" He holds it out. "This sword slices up dragons like butter! We actually HAVE a chance!"

"I didn't know," you say, but you already know it's too late. You've already made your choice. Taking a battle stance, you sigh. "Arlaka... "

"Yeah yeah," the orc says dismissively. "I know. Let's do this, traitor." He readies his sword. "Don't think I'll go easy on you just because I knew you before. I am the hero our people need in this time of darkness!" he boasts. "Get ready."

Enter 'C' to continue.
> C
Battle
You can check your Enemy and Yourself.
You can try to flee by going out.

You are fighting against Arlaka! He wields a strange, blood red sword with the word 'Drachenölő' etched on it. He's also wearing a set of radiant armor, burning so bright it's hard to look at.

Enter 'C' to continue.
> C
Arlaka...

Looking down at the collapsed orc, you sigh. "Oh Arlaka..." His Phoenix Armor has been extinguished, bent and broken of its power so that's it's nothing more than scrap metal. His own sword lies buried in his chest. You killed him. You killed him. It was your duty. He was your friend. Oh by the gods, you killed him...

You stand there in shock, Bovek burning around you, until someone swoops down beside you. "Kai," Corona says. "What are you... oh my." Corona cranes his neck over and stares at Arlaka's body. "That armor... good heavens, that sword. Kai, are you alright?" Still in shock, you can only manage to nod. "You... beat him. Do you understand what that means?" Corona grins slightly, showing off the tips of his fangs. "That is the sword Drachenölő, made specifically to assault our kind. Its magic would've hurt you yourself quite badly, too. Kai, you're a hero for this. So many of my kind would have, probably have already, fallen to that blade. Come." He scoops up the blade in his claws, and if it burns him like it burned you he doesn't show it. "Let's get you out of this city," the dragon says, stepping towards you.

You're about to ask what he means by that, when he rears up on his hind legs, flaps his wings, and starts hovering. You're right beneath his golden underbelly scales, and then he lashes out with his hind legs and wraps the sickle claws around you. You shout in protest, but allow him to carry you off.

The world around you shakes and sways from side to side sickeningly. The city of Bovek passes away from beneath you, and then you're sailing over night-shrouded plains. At one point, you lose your lunch to the land far below. The tune of Corona's beating wings and the rhythm of his swaying tail continues until stone enters your view. Turning your head, you're surprised to see Mt. Vulcis up close and personal. A few dragons are patrolling the airspace around it, little more than black dots.

Then Corona's bringing you through a tunnel, and his grip on you tightens. For a sickening second, his wings stop beating and he plummets down a long corridor of carved stone, before extending them again and gliding to safety. Then the tunnel ends, and you get to see the insides of Mt. Vulcis.

It's far from what you expected. The mountain is hollow, supported structurally by some unimaginable magic. Structures line the interior walls, light shines down from the top, and hundreds of tunnels scurry about the base of the mountain. In the center of it all is a giant cylindrical structure of gold and silver. It is to a tunnel that leads there that Corona flies.

Soon you're above the stone path, and Corona slows to a hover, wingbeats filling the air like drums. "Alright, I'm letting go of you. Brace yourself!" You do. Then his claws let go and you fall the short distane to the ground. Your legs, numb from the long flight, instantly give way and you collapse with a strangled sound. Corona lands beside you, Drachenölő still in his grip. "Sorry," he apologizes.

"I'm fine," you say, getting up on shaking limbs. "What are we doing here?"

"Well, you might not have known this but I'm... actually rather high up in the ranks of Her Majesty. Lithoa's my subordinate, for instance. I report directly to Queen Aerona herself, and given your act of heroism, above and beyond the call of duty in facing down, and defeating no less, the wielder of Drachenölő I think she'll want to meet you." You go still, blinking up at the dragon next to you. He tries to smile reassuringly. "Relax. Queen Aerona is very kind. I know she started this war, but believe me when I say she truly doesn't hold your people in contempt. Just act natural."

With that, Corona walks to the end of the path, where there's a large wooden door. He pushes it in with his head and you follow behind him, trembling. Time to meet the Queen of the Dragons.

Enter 'C' to continue.
> C
Queen of Dragons

Your breath catches in your throat.

The building's interior is made of gold and silver, with elaborate balconies and chandeliers. Stylized pictures of dragons midflight cover the walls. Dragon sized tables and, to your surprise, regular sized ones also fill the ground. Four heavy doors lead out from the ground level, complete with golden handles. The cylindrical room is large enough to let a dragon fly laps with room to spare. It didn't look as large from the outside.

There are already two dragons here. One is an indigo wyrm, with tattered wings and a chipped tail bone, and the other is a colossal gray dragoness, sleek and angular like a snake, who is twice the size of any other dragon you've ever seen. She has golden jewelry wrapped around her horns. The two dragons are turned to each other, busy discussing something in the Draconic tongue.

Corona clears his throat, and both the indigo dragon's red eyes and the gray dragoness's steely blue eyes fall upon the two of you. "My Queen," he says in Common, bowing his head low. "I hope I am not disturbing you." Uncertain, you also fall into a bow.

"No, it is fine," the colossal dragon chimes. "Please rise, both of you." She pads over two you, her claws clicking on the marble floor. "Corona, the Wildfire. I am surprised to see you here, so soon after a succesful mission."

He nods. "Kai here has done our people a great service." He holds out his claw and drops the sword to the ground. It clatters deafeningly. "He did battle with the wielder of Drachenölő and, on his own, defeated him. He shattered the power of the Phoenix Armor and brought Drachenölő to us. I felt this was news fitting to deliver to your Highness," Corona explains.

The indigo dragon rolls his eyes, weaving to Aerona's side. "How fascinating. So the mortal managed not to be killed by a weapon designed to kill dragons. My Queen," he begins, looking up at the gray dragon. "You are not seriously going to waste your time on this one, are you?"

"Hush, Calascope," Aerona says. "Overcoming Drachenölő is no small task, even for a Wyrmblood. Listen, the expeditions have returned enough reagents, haven't they?" she asks.

The newly christened Calascope looks at the dragon worriedly. "Yes, they have. More than enough, actually."

The Queen nods. "Then go unleash the Corinaders. Drachenölő may be out of the equation, but our people will still die out there regardless. It's time to bring this war to a close." She sighs sadly and looks at the floor. "Just... get it over with. Please."

He sighs and relaxes his wings. "Very well. I shall take my leave, Aerona." The indigo dragon flaps his wings and takes off, exiting through one of the holes in the roof.

Aerona looks back at the two of you. "I'm sorry about that. Corona, why don't you return to the camp? I shall send... Kai was it? Kai after you once I have spoken with him." Wait. She wants to speak to you alone? The Queen of all Dragons wants to speak to you... alone?

Corona bows. "Of course." He looks at you once and winks reassuringly, though with how reptilian his eye is it's not as calming as he probably would've liked. "Thank you for indulging my sudden intrusion."

Aerona waves a wing at him, and the spotless appendage is so massive it booms through the air. "Of course." Then Corona's gone, and you're alone with Aerona. "Please young one, bring that sword here." You eye the sword Drachenölő nervously, but you're not going to say 'no' to the queen of all dragons' face. You grip the sword's handle, wincing as it prickles against your skin even through your equipment.

Approaching Aerona makes her seem even bigger than before. Her scales are polished and smooth, not a single one out of place. Her gleaming claws are filed down to points. Her muscles are tightly bunched underneath her flesh, rippling and bulging. Her chest scales steadily expand in and out as she takes breaths into her cavernous lungs.

You look down at Drachenölő and your thoughts turn treasonous. A sword designed to kill dragons. And here is the leader of the dragons, undefended, right within striking range. You could do it, strike her down and return to Engim, to Bovek, to Elizabeth as a hero, with everything forgiven.

... or you could stick to your vows and simply speak with her.

Enter 'Q' to strike Aerona with Drachenölő.

Enter 'C' to continue without striking her.
> C
Queen of Dragons

You decide to leave it be. You're too far into this to turn away now.

A tornado forms around Drachenölő and wrenches it from your grip. Aerona casually levitates the sword to her head, eying it all the while. With an audible snap the winds around Drachenölő shear against it. The sword snaps like a toothpick, releasing a flash of arcane light before the shards of metal fall to the ground. "It is done," she intones, bowing her head and closing her eyes.

After a moment, the Queen shakes herself and focuses on you.

"You did very well," Aerona continues. "Your battle against the wielder of Drachenölő  must've been treachorous. You deserve compensation. As such, I shall bestow upon you a boon." Silver streamers of light form and wrap around Aerona, giving her an otherworldly appearance. You step back in alarm as she extends her wings, and the arcane energies instantly flow into you. You feel... you feel...

Maximum Mana increased by 300!

"There," she says, ending the flow of magic. "I think that's all I can safely give you." Her eyes soften. "But please. Do not think I am congratulating you on your service by making you more capable of further service. This leads to the second part of my boon." Aerona raises her head and places a foreleg on her chest. "As long as you live, my lair shall be open to you. Should you ever need anything at all, my home is your home. And after this war ends, if you should require it, I will be happy to house you, young one."

"Thank you," you say faintly. "If... that is all you wish of me, Queen Aerona?" you ask.

She smiles at you, not showing off any fangs. "Do as you will. But please, do not feel as if you cannot approach my people. The species barrier does not go so far as to forbid certain individuals from interacting."

You really hope you never piss her off.

You're in a corridor. It's dragon sized, so the golden arches seem to take up the entire sky. You idly wonder how such a structure could fit anywhere, even underground. The floor is marble tiled, the designs are exquisite. At the end is a colossal wooden door, slammed shut. Golden light illuminates the scene from an unknown, magical source. But the most striking feature of the corridor are the windows. Stained glass windows, flush against the surrounding stone, each with an engraving underneath.

The window closest to you shows three mortal wizards, clad in robes and with closed eyes, standing side by side with their arms out. Beneath them is an eggshell shape of yellow light, and inside that, countless dragons.

The engraving reads, "Imprisonment".

Long Corridor
Your attention is drawn to a Stained Glass Window.
You can go south or north.

You're in a corridor. It's dragon sized, so the golden arches seem to take up the entire sky. You idly wonder how such a structure could fit anywhere, even underground. The floor is marble tiled, the designs are exquisite. At the end is a colossal wooden door, slammed shut. Golden light illuminates the scene from an unknown, magical source. But the most striking feature of the corridor are the windows. Stained glass windows, flush against the surrounding stone, each with an engraving underneath.

The window closest to you shows a war. Armies of humans, elves and orcs, as well as other mortals you don't recognize, line up against what can only be demons. They're wretched creatures of incredibly varying body structure. Some have six arms. Others are muscular brutes. Some have dragon wings, others have horns. Some float, some walk on all fours, others on two legs. All of them have skin as black as tar. Above the mortal army an enormous gray dragon, who can only be Queen Aerona, snarls down at the demons. One of the mortals is wielding the unmistakable form of Drachenölő.

The engraving reads, "Effort".
> go south
Long Corridor
Your attention is drawn to a Stained Glass Window.
You can go south or north.

You're in a corridor. It's dragon sized, so the golden arches seem to take up the entire sky. You idly wonder how such a structure could fit anywhere, even underground. The floor is marble tiled, the designs are exquisite. At the end is a colossal wooden door, slammed shut. Golden light illuminates the scene from an unknown, magical source. But the most striking feature of the corridor are the windows. Stained glass windows, flush against the surrounding stone, each with an engraving underneath.

The window closest to you shows grassy plains rupturing, a putrid black rift opening in the middle, and tiny creatures - just specks from this distance - crawling out from it.

The engraving reads, "Invasion".

> speak to general bijorn
Protecting Mt. Vulcis

Once again, you find yourself sitting in the temple, surrounded by the ranks of the Wyrmblood. The sun shines through the window, casting long shadows against the walls. It's eerily quiet as General Bijorn takes the stage.

"Thank you all for joining me. Now, good news. This war is going in our favor. The Corinaders have been unleashed. As they outnumbered and overwhelmed the demons in ages past, now they shall do the same to those who would deny the dragons the world they once protected. We, the faithful, shall be spared their wrath. It seems we've won."

Cheers start to fill the room, but Bijorn is immediately raising his hand to calm everyone. "However, there is one small detail. The Corinaders, as magical constructs, come with a shutdown spell. If the mortals were to get their hands on it, then the Corinaders would be powerless against them. Furthermore, as most of the dragons are already being evacuated back to Mt. Vulcis to ride out the storm, there is reason to believe the mortals will attempt to seal them away once more."

Murmurs fill the room. Seal the dragons away? "Movement has been detected. It is believed the mortal armies are initiating an all-in strike. They will break through Mt. Vulcis's main entrance and send in spies to locate the shutdown spell, which is hidden away within the mountain, but on hand in case the Corinaders go berserk like they did last time. It is believed their highest wizards have convened and recreated the sealing spell, and intend to cast it on Mt. Vulcis while the majority of dragonkind are within. It is our belief that the dragons will be able to shatter this barrier within a month this time around. We are not going to risk it. We will stop them from breaking into Mt. Vulcis, we will find their wizards and end them, all while Corinaders swarm the enemy!" he shouts, pumping a fist.

So does everyone else. Awkwardly, and feeling entirely out of place, you join in.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
You're traveling to Mt. Vulcis by way of a pallet again. This time, you're being flown in by a yellow dragon named Tlinder. It's quiet all around.

The wooden walls of the pallet are partially folded down, though, and with how close you're seated to them you can see the battlefield outside.

Mt. Vulcis isn't exactly surrounded, but a good quarter of the mountain is engulfed in turmoil. Humans, elves and orcs pile the ground in all sorts of formations. Arrows, magicians, warriors, berserkers...

The biggest feature of the mortal army are the siege engines. They're enormous metal constructs fastened to the earth, no doubt packed with engineers. Even as you watch, they catapult a slew of boulders through the contraptions atop them, which sail to Mt. Vulcis and impact against an enormous wall of stone sealing off its main passage.

Mt. Vulcis itself is surrounded by a thin ripple. It makes it look as though the mountain is surrounded with a thin film of soap. It's the magical barrier being summoned by the wizards, and for a moment you fear it's already completed.

But even if it isn't, it doesn't matter. You still have a job. A job which, you suspect, will be made tremendously easier by the thousands of Corinaders storming the battlefield.

You've never seen them before, and they're not at all what you expected. The Corinaders, the feared magical constructs that overwhelmed the demons and nearly eradicated all intelligent life afterward... are boxes. Floating purple, cubical boxes about the size of your torso, with a metallic sheen to them. On one face, the Corinaders each have a single eye, which rapidly flits in all directions. The other sides have tentacles. Each side sprouts one, giving each Corinader a total of five.

Even as you watch, swarms of Corinaders attack the mortals on the ground by extending their tentacles, which telescope out to surprising distances to impale with their sharp ends. You also see a fireball crash into a Corinader. It goes limp and starts to fall, but boils away into a dense fog before it even hits the ground. Still elsewhere, you watch one kill somebody, then take a moment to literally split into two copies of itself. On the horizon, all around Mt. Vulcis, are colossal purple spiderwebs seemingly suspended in the air. You'd guess they're Corinader nests.

Dragons are a surprising rarity around the dragon's home. A few of them fly around, pelting elemental blasts into the army below, but it seems they're mostly content with having the Corinaders - and the Wyrmblood, you think irately - do the heavy lifting. You suppose if they really are endangered it makes sense...

Tlinder lands, and your pallet box opens up. You stand on instinct and rush out into the battlefield. Disable the siege engines, Bijorn said. Disable the siege engines so the mortals can't get inside the mountain to get the Corinader shutdown spell.

You look around... you're already separated from the others, lost in a sea of battling mortals. You gulp, but get ready to fight!

Enter 'C' to continue.
> C
Mt. Vulcis
You can go northwest, northeast or south.

It's a war zone here! In the distance is Mt. Vulcis, slowly being sealed away under a shimmering, magical barrier. All around you, mortals are fighting against the Wyrmblood while Corinaders fly about overhead, trading fire with bowmen and sorcerers. The air is filled with deafening thuds as Siege Engines catapult boulders towards Mt. Vulcis's main entrance, threatening to collapse it inward.

There are so many people here, it's hard to tell which way to go!

The air is filled with deafening thuds as Siege Engines catapult boulders towards Mt. Vulcis's main entrance, threatening to collapse it inward.

There are so many people here, it's hard to tell which way to go!

You can go east.

It's a war zone here! In the distance is Mt. Vulcis, slowly being sealed away under a shimmering, magical barrier. All around you, mortals are fighting against the Wyrmblood while Corinaders fly about overhead, trading fire with bowmen and sorcerers. The air is filled with deafening thuds as Siege Engines catapult boulders towards Mt. Vulcis's main entrance, threatening to collapse it inward.

There are so many people here, it's hard to tell which way to go!

> C
Last Resort, Repelled

You leap off the final siege engine as it shudders to a hault, its catapult falling to the ground. Panting heavily, you watch as it shuts down.

And you aren't the only one who's been destroying them. The dozens of siege engines all around are falling silent as the Wyrmblood focus them down, leaving the mortals to the mercy of the swarming clouds of Corinaders. Is it just you, or are there... more of them than before?

You look around, searching for your next target. The magical boxes sail by you, eerily silent and in utter disregard of your existence. The mortals' ranks are collapsing, and your heart aches. Could Arlaka have saved them? In any case, it's much too late for regrets.

There doesn't even seem to be anything you can do. Or indeed, any of the Wyrmblood. The dragons that had flown in are still circling high above, safe. The siege engines have been dismantled, so there's no way for any mortal to get inside Mt. Vulcis and steal the shutdown spell. Your eyes follow the shifting swarms of Corinaders, and you end up staring at a hidden, out-of-the-way crevice where a tremendous amount of magic is spilling. It lights up the stone and sky... but then the floating boxes converge on it and the light slowly dies off. Once it's dim, it gives out all at once.

A wave of pressure nearly knocks you off your feet. You're not alone, everyone around you is rocked back. Even the Corinaders aren't immune to the brief wind. A look behind you confirms it: the bubble that had been forming around Mt. Vulcis is gone, the spell interrupted.

You've won.

You stand numbly as the Corinaders finish striking down survivors, after which they depart, floating emotionlessly back to their nests. Slowly but surely, you and the other Wyrmblood come together in a group, pushing aside the bodies - oh heavens so many bodies, they're all dead, the flies are already coming, the very ground runs red. You take a deep breath and focus, wrenching your gaze away even as your arms start to shake violently.

Eventually, your camp of Wyrmblood are all together, staring at the bleak battlefield. One of the dragons above comes spiraling down, and soon resolves into the golden form of Corona. He lands hard, carving a shallow groove in the earth with his talons. Once he comes to a halt he faces your way and addresses the few dozen of you.

"Well, I must admit I was far more worried about this than I should have been. Look at you all!" He waves a wing. "Not a single casaulty, that is unheard of. General Bijorn will be overjoyed. Matalin, excellent work on coralling the Corinaders to desired points." An elven woman looks away. "And Kai, great work discovering the siege engines' weaknesses." You shuffle your feet awkwardly. How do you respond to that? "This war is mostly over at this point. It is high time that you all begin figuring out what you shall do in the new world, once the Corinaders..." He frowns deeply. "... finish their work, and eradicate themselves. Come, it's time to go home," he says, gesturing with a wing towards the wooden pallets you had arrived in.

Nodding, you slowly turn around and head towards them, your fellow Wyrmblood around you. The air reeks of death and despair. Your heart is heavy. The mortals failed to get the shutdown spell. The wizards failed to seal away the dragons. Now the Corinaders will run increasingly out of control. Your people will be destroyed, with nothing but the sterile Wyrmblood as your last gasps. The knowledge that the dragons will attempt to keep your customs and ways alive long after humans, elves, and orcs are gone... somehow makes it worse.

The trip back to the camp is in grave silence.

Enter 'C' to continue.

Wyrmblood Clinic
You can go south.

You are in the camp of the Wyrmblood.

You spend some time getting checked up and medicated.

Health and Mana restored!

This is a cross between an infirmary and lost and found. Several unused cots line the walls, and a pile of assorted belongings is pushed into the corner. You can leave by going south.

> go south
Wyrmblood Camp
You can go west, east, north or up.

You are in the camp of the Wyrmblood.

Wyrmblood, mortals who have joined the dragons in exchange for being spared, roam around in their glowing armor. Tents and small buildings are scattered around the area, a strange cross between permanent and makeshift. From here, you can go west or east. You can also go north to the Wyrmblood infirmary/lost-and-found. There's also a path up, which leads to Mt. Vulcis.

Corinaders fill the air, sailing above your head. They don't make moves towards any of you, though, and they're not trying to turn the camp into a nest. Still more than a little eerie, though, given how preternaturally silent they are.

Appearance
You can go east.

Your name is Kai Trinnal.

You are a Human man in your mid thirties, and you feel you've done fairly well for your age. Your body is stocky and strong, with muscles built up from long years working as your village's blacksmith. You stand at a solid 165 centimeters, when you aren't wearing shoes.

Your hair is brown, like hot cocoa, and is currently cut short. Your eyes aren't your most remarkable feature, being just a steady blue, and your ears are average shells as is normal for your race. Your smile is wide and only slightly crooked.

Your skin is a light tannish brown, scorched by the sun.

On your head, you are wearing a Wyrmblood Helmet.

On your chest, you are wearing a Wyrmblood Chestplate.

On your legs, you are wearing Wyrmblood Leggings.

On your feet, you are wearing Wyrmblood Boots.

Your hands grip a Shimmering Sword.

Warrior

'W': Slam: Deals your Strength * 3 damage to your enemy, but also deals 5 damage to yourself. This damage cannot kill you. Diminished by enemy Armor and Constitution.

'E': Blood Rage: Costs 5 mana. Deals your Magic Power * 5 damage to your enemy and reduces their Constitution by 5. Damage diminished by enemy Willpower and Spell Resistance.

Passive: Executioner: When your opponent is at 25% or less health, you deal 30% more damage.

Appearance
You can go east.

Your name is Kai Trinnal.

You are a Human man in your mid thirties, and you feel you've done fairly well for your age. Your body is stocky and strong, with muscles built up from long years working as your village's blacksmith. You stand at a solid 165 centimeters, when you aren't wearing shoes.

Your hair is brown, like hot cocoa, and is currently cut short. Your eyes aren't your most remarkable feature, being just a steady blue, and your ears are average shells as is normal for your race. Your smile is wide and only slightly crooked.

Your skin is a light tannish brown, scorched by the sun.

On your head, you are wearing a Wyrmblood Helmet.

On your chest, you are wearing a Wyrmblood Chestplate.

On your legs, you are wearing Wyrmblood Leggings.

On your feet, you are wearing Wyrmblood Boots.

Your hands grip a Shimmering Sword.
