Wyrmblood Camp
You can go west, south or north.

You are in the camp of the Wyrmblood.

Wyrmblood, mortals who have joined the dragons in exchange for being spared, roam around in their glowing armor. Tents and small buildings are scattered around the area, a strange cross between permanent and makeshift. From here, you can go south, west, or east. You can also go north to the Wyrmblood apothecary. There's also a path up, which leads to Mt. Vulcis.

Corinaders fill the air, sailing above your head. They don't make moves towards any of you, though, and they're not trying to turn the camp into a nest. Still more than a little eerie, though, given how preternaturally silent they are.

Wyrmblood, mortals who have joined the dragons in exchange for being spared, roam around in their glowing armor. > go up
Mt. Vulcis
You can go south or down.

You're standing on a stone ledge, overlooking the countryside surrounding Mt. Vulcis. It's eerily quiet, save for the occasional purple structure in the distance. A Corinader nest.

You can go south, towards the cavern that leads into Mt. Vulcis. There's also a path down the mountainside that leads to your Wyrmblood Camp.

Corinaders nests dot the land. The nests are enormous purple spiderwebs, with their lower edges attached to the ground and the upper ends simply suspended in the air. The nests are slightly blurry, as even as you watch more and more Corinaders form from them and leave, filling the sky with millions of the things.

Mt. Vulcis

You're in the cavern mouth, just inside Mt. Vulcis. It's as silent as the grave.

You can go south, deeper into Mt. Vulcis. You can also go north, outside.

Mt. Vulcis
Leading into the earth is a Mining Elevator.
You can go north or down.

You're deep inside Mt. Vulcis. Water drips from stalactites on the ceiling. It's as silent as the grave. There's a mining elevator here, probably left over from ancient times and repurposed for Wyrmblood use. It'll likely take you deep into the mountain.

You can go north to head out of the mountain, or down the mining elevator into the depths of Mt. Vulcis.
> go down
Dragons' Lair
Watching you carefully are members of the a Draconic Guard.
You can go up or down.

You're standing in a vast atrium. There's a mining elevator to bring you up out of the earth. There's also a door here that leads down into the earth, toward Aerona's chambers.

Four dragons, one of each type, are guarding it. They're the first dragons you've ever seen wearing armor, resplendant and bronze. They give you acknowledging glances as they stand vigil over the door.
> go down
Long Corridor
Your attention is drawn to a Stained Glass Window.
You can go up or north.

You're in a corridor. It's dragon sized, so the golden arches seem to take up the entire sky. You idly wonder how such a structure could fit anywhere, even underground. The floor is marble tiled, the designs are exquisite. At the end is a colossal wooden door, slammed shut. Golden light illuminates the scene from an unknown, magical source. But the most striking feature of the corridor are the windows. Stained glass windows, flush against the surrounding stone, each with an engraving underneath.

The window closest to you shows four dragons near the top, and at the bottom are an elf, human, and orc, facing the dragons. There are other races too, ones you don't recognize. They're all wearing brown rags.

The engraving reads, "Discovery".

> go north
Queen's Chamber
Sitting patiently in the exact center of the room is Queen Aerona.
You can go north, east, west or south.

Aerona's chambers are as oppulant as they are huge. Patterned with silver and gold, balconies, pictures of dragons in flight, chandeliers, the list goes on. The floor is marbled tile and clicks beneath your steps.

Aerona is here, looking over some documents. Her claws tap against the marble floor patiently. Sitting on her haunches, Aerona's wings form a cocoon against her back and her tail is up in the air, swaying back and forth. She's huge, twice the size of any other dragon. You really hope you never piss her off.
> speak to aerona

"Ah, hello young one. It pains me to ask of this of you, but I have a request. But please, do make sure you are fully prepared and have nothing left undone. Talk to me again when you are ready."

(It is recommended to save here)
> speak to aerona
Queen Aerona's Mission

Aerona looks... ancient when you approach her again. She sits with her wings flat against the ground and her long, swanlike neck bent to the point where her head nearly touches the floor. She looks nothing like the image of the Queen of the mighty, proud and immortal dragons. She looks nothing like the monarch who ordered the eradication of three species. In the cavernous depths of her chamber, she just looks like a child being scolded, scales and claws aside.

"Thank you for coming," she says, her rumbling accent clear as day. "I'm sure you already know how things are..." She waves her left wing vaguely. "Outside. The Corinaders were able to outnumber even the demons. Much as it pains me to say, your people stand no chance." Aerona narrows the scaly ridges over her blue eyes. "Or so I thought. There is something going on in the city of Bovek. They should be utterly consumed by now, yet they are holding on due to some magic that repels the constructs."

"Some sort of barrier?" you guess.

Aerona shakes her head. "Nothing so visible. It's more like... a repellent." She stands up on all fours - oh hell she's enormous, even among dragons! - and raises her head proudly. "If there is even a chance this effect can be duplicated, it must be stopped. I do not believe, however, that it is anything obvious. Not something that can simply be smashed." Aerona smiles kindly at you, showing no fangs. "That is where you come in. You are relatively small, and quiet. You are incredibly skilled too, as evidenced by overcoming both Drachenölő and the Phoenix Armor at the same time. I wish for you to investigate Bovek, locate whatever is holding the Corinaders at bay, and put an end to it. Then return here, and speak to me as to what has transpired."

She pads closer to you, her footsteps shaking the earth. Aerona reaches a paw to nearly cup you, but politely refrains from actually touching you. "But understand! This is not me, as ruler of my kind, demanding this of you. This is purely a request. If you do this you will be risking your life for my people once again, and it's not even uncertain the issue cannot be resolved otherwise. If need be, I myself can strap on my armor and deal with the issue personally, and trust me when I say I would be in far less danger than yourself." She exhales sharply through her nostrils. "Were I not queen, I would be doing so right now."

"I'll do it," you say, instantly regretting it. "I made my vows, and I'll see it through." You take a deep breath to calm your nerves. "If you truly think I am the best person for this job, I'll do it. Just tell me where to go."

Aerona pulls away from you in shock, but nods stoically. "Very well. One of my kind shall fly you into the city. From the air, you are to locate the area of repellment, determine the source, and if possible, disable it. Then please, come back. You have done more than enough. Go prepare. You are dismissed, Kai."

A little shaken and more than a little nervous, you turn around to go prepare.

Enter 'C' to continue.
> C
Mystery of Bovek

A while later, you're on your way to Bovek, atop the back of a gray dragon assigned to you. He called himself Nimbolus, and promptly refused to speak the rest of the trip. Your reasonable request - a saddle - was shot down too, with some nonsense explanation about how dragons were not beasts of burden, and that you should be happy Nimbolus was even letting you ride on his back, rather than be carried in his claws.

So here you are, flying through the air on the back of a sleet-gray wyrm, whose scaly spine rises and falls rhythmically beneath you. Your entire lower body aches: you don't even want to imagine how much this is going to hurt later. But as Nimbolus flies you away from Mt. Vulcis, you find that you can't focus much on the pain, or even on the mission. Because all around you is the end of the world.

You thought there were a lot of Corinaders when you fought around Mt. Vulcis. But this is ridiculous!

You're being flown through a literal cloud. In the time it took you to go from the camp, into Mt. Vulcis and then fly out, the Corinaders spread this far. They make way for Nimbolus, swerving away as he flies through the storm. It's not that the Corinaders form a dense, impassible wall. But there's so many that your eyes hurt just looking at them, and the horizon is obscured in a tidal wave of violet. Here and there you can see their enormous nests sprawling across the ground, and from the nests' webbing you see more and more Corinaders form and join the swarm.

What's going through the minds of the people of the world? Watching the oncoming tide? Do they know what it is? Are they going to fight? Are they spending their last moments with their families? Your throat tightens at the thought.

Soon, Nimbolus has brought you to Bovek and approaches downtown, circling about. The city is almost unrecognizable. The outer reaches of the city have been smashed to rubble. Corinaders swarm around the city like a tornado, kicking up such a gale you fear you'll be torn from your escort's back. Near the center of the city you can see the Cathedral's spires, standing tall and proud despite the storm. Near the base, you can see mortals fighting, firing arrows and magic into the storm in a vain effort to hold them off.

Interestingly enough though... the mortals themselves are fine. The Corinaders aren't approaching the Cathedral. That must be it. That's what Aerona was talking about.

"Over there!" you shout over the background noise. While the magic constructs themselves are silent, the noise they make as they bump into each other, as they attack, or as they fall to the ground, is stunning. "Get me over there! The tall building!"

"The Cathedral, I see it little one," Nimbolus says, circling lower. You look closer at the Cathedral. Sure enough, while the Corinaders are spilling through the streets like a swarm of bees, they form a bubble around the Cathedral, letting the defenders within mount a last, desperate defense, even as the outskirts of the city are already being covered in the webbing of Corinader nests.

Then one of the defenders looks up and sees Nimbolus. He must look like a shadow in the storm, but recognizable as a dragon all the same. The wyrm turns his head to you as the mortal runs into the Cathedral. "Child, what are they doing?" he asks.

Then the person runs back out, with a long metal tube in hand. You frown. What is that? You've never seen anything...

Suddenly the nozzle is pointed at Nimbolus, and you understand. "Run!" you shout. Nimbolus wheels around in the air, and you have to wrap your legs tightly around his neck - even though they don't go all the way around - just to hang on. Something fiery, crackling with magic and moving too fast to see, shoots past you and straight into the webbing of Nimbolus's wings.

His roar pierces the sky, and his thrashing sends you flying through the air! Buildings, Corinaders, and the dragon's shadow tumble in and out of your view as you fall and fall...

Enter 'C' to continue!
> C
Vengeance

"Oof!" You just collided against the side of one of the Cathedral's spires. Without a moment to get your footing you start rolling down, until you come to rest on something hard and cold.

Surprisingly enough you land not on the ground, but the roof. Standing up, you look around in confusion. You're on top of Bovek's Cathedral, a square piece of roof surrounded by the church's stone spires. There's a trapdoor that leads below, but it looks to be locked. The floor is stone, dusty and covered in natural residue.

You hope Nimbolus got away, but you're sure he'll be fine. Now, you need to get off this roof...

"Hello, Kai," someone says. You spin around and there, standing on the roof with you, is Elizabeth Gray. She's wearing her standard plate armor, sans helmet, so her steely eyes are easily visible. Her brown hair flutters in the Corinader-induced breeze. A two-handed sword is strapped to her waist. "It's been a while, hasn't it?" she asks, absolutely no inflection in her voice.

"Yes, it has," you say warily. "Elizabeth, look. I'm sorry about all this, but please. You can come with me!" you say, stowing your weapon and holding out your hand, taking a step towards the woman. "You can join the Wyrmblood, you don't need to die."

"Die," she mutters, looking at the air around you. Betentacled boxes darken the sky, forming a very firm boundary around the building. "Yes, we're going to die, aren't we? It's too late. We could barely handle dragons, let alone Corinaders too. They're creations of dark magic, they won't come near this building... but I suspect even that won't last forever. We're all going to die. Because you betrayed us. You killed Arlaka, our only hope." She laughs bitterly. "I should've killed you long ago. And sadly, everyone else is busy downstairs, so..."

"You don't have to die, though! I came here with a dragon, you can make the pact here! The Corinaders will ignore you then," you promise. But Elizabeth doesn't seem to hear you.

"You know," Elizabeth begins in a flat voice. "I think I understand why you joined the dragons." She looks around the battlefield. Millions of Corinaders fill the air, seemingly ignorant of your existence. "You didn't want to face this. You wanted to live. You joined them because you wanted to survive this war." She looks back at you and grins. "Which is funny! Because now? You're going to die anyway."

You stare at her. "Elizabeth, please," you warn. "Don't make me do this."

Silently, she pulls out her sword and grips it tightly.

You nod slowly and ready your own weapon. "Alright. And Elizabeth, for what it's worth?" You sigh. "I'm sorry."

She stares at you grimly. "You will be."

The gray irises of Elizabeth's eyes suddenly glow brilliant green, emerald smoke spilling from them. She points her sword at you and the same green smoke snakes along it, before abruptly turning dark red at the tip.

Suddenly pain envelops your being and you fall to the ground, breathing heavily. It feels as though fire is in your blood, like a vice clenches your heart. You stare up at Elizabeth, thoughts racing as your vision blurs. "You... what did you do?"

"A little disease I cooked up long ago," she says coldly, the light in her eyes dying. "Like it?"

"You're... you're a Plaguebearer," you realize, struggling to stand.

"That's right, I am," she confirms. "I promised long ago I'd never use my powers to hurt anyone. But I'm making an exception for you, you son of a bitch!"

You've been afflicted with the Blood Plague! Periodically losing Health!

The Last Plaguebearer

Eyes glassy and unfocused, Elizabeth sways to the side and collapses, breathing weakly. Her sword clatters against the ground and she raises an armored hand at you, scowling despite the blood trailing from her mouth. She cups her palm and, shaking heavily, points it at you. Her eyes glow and emerald fog collects in her hand...

... but then she rolls over onto her back, the mist and glow both die out, and she breathes her last.

You did it. You beat Elizabeth Gray.

Enter 'C' to continue.
> C
Central Roof
 Elizabeth Gray.
You can go northwest, north, northeast, east, west, southwest, south or southeast.

You're on top of Bovek Cathedral. You're in the very center of the roof.

The ground is cracked and cratered from Elizabeth's lightning, parts of it pulvurized into sand and then melted back into glass. A fine green mist covers the slabs of rock, residue of her plague magic.

The sky is red with fire and smoke. All around you, Corinaders hover through the air by the hundreds, their tendrils striking any enemies.

Elizabeth is here, dead on the ground. Her armor's crumpled and torn, her hair's matted with sweat and her skin is clammy. Her open eyes stare blankly into the distance, filled with hate. A pool of blood darkens the land around her.
> go north
Northern Roof
You can go west, southwest, south, southeast, east or north.

You're on top of Bovek Cathedral. You're in the Northern part of the roof.

The ground is cracked and cratered from Elizabeth's lightning, parts of it pulvurized into sand and then melted back into glass. A fine green mist covers the slabs of rock, residue of her plague magic.

The sky is red with fire and smoke. All around you, Corinaders hover through the air by the hundreds, their tendrils striking any enemies.

You can try going north to get the attention of Nimbolus, and get the hell off this roof.
> go north
Extraction

Breathing heavily, you stumble around the rooftop, looking for a way down. You can't just go to ground level. For starters, the fall's tremendous. Normally you could, with your enchanted gear, survive that. But Elizabeth's disease magic has you feeling frail and weak, as if a feather could knock you over. That drop will kill you.

Going down the ladder isn't an option either. The rest of the mortals are still down there, and while they won't recognize you, they'll realize you weren't with them before, and you seriously doubt you could fight them off now. The only way you're getting out of Bovek is on dragonback, and the only dragon for miles around is Nimbolus.

If he doesn't show up, then what? You'll be forced to stay on this rooftop indefinitely. Even if you did convince the people here not to kill you, how much food do they have? How long will the store of Holy Water last? You shiver at the thought of having come so far, defeating a Plaguebearer in single combat, only to shrivel away on this church.

It gets harder to breathe. You take your helmet off and throw it to the ground. Its magic instantly fades from your body and you gasp for breath as you weaken further, but you can at least take proper breaths now.

The Corinaders are still circling. How could you possibly grab Nimbolus's attention, if he even is around? If he even can fly? No, no. That doesn't matter. You have to at least try to find him.

You walk around the roof, peering into the depths of the swarm. Corinaders rush along by the thousands, spilling through the streets and weaving through the air, spiraling around the Cathedral. You keep looking for any indication of a dragon... wait. There! A flash of gray on the distant rooftops, barely visible between the tide of floating boxes.

"HEY!" you shout so loudly your head buzzes, facing the glimmer of gray. You wave your weapon through the air. "Over here!" You don't even care if the people down below hear you. They're probably busy anyway. Hopping around, you follow the sliver of gray as it moves across the rooftops, growing... larger! Larger means he's getting closer!

Soon Nimbolus actually becomes visible, crawling across the rooftops while using his claws as hooks. His left wing has an enormous hole in its webbing. Yikes. If the mortals had had weapons like THAT before the Corinaders were unleashed...

Nimbolus pierces the bubble of Corinaders, parting them as he approaches. He stares at you and blinks in shock, pulling his neck back. "Mortal! You're alive!"

"Get over here before they kill me!" you shout desperately. A spell of dizziness overtakes you and you stumble. Even after you've quieted down your head's still buzzing. The ongoing noise of the Corinaders doesn't make it any better.

Nimbolus extends his left wing, showing off the hole. "I can't fly, so I'll have to climb! Hold on!" Without further ado Nimbolus leaps off the buildings, his hind legs leaving craters in their walls. He stalks across the street to the Cathedral's base, clear of the Corinaders, and starts climbing it like a giant, winged cat. Breathing heavily, you sit on the ground with one hand braced against the stone. It's warm to the touch, cracked and seared by Elizabeth's lightning.

Soon, Nimbolus appears between the Cathedral's spires. From where you're sitting he looks even larger than normal. "Oh my, what happened here?" he asks, inspecting the battlefield. Elizabeth catches his eye. "Who is she?"

A friend? A Plaguebearer who tried to kill you? "Someone I knew before I switched sides. She uh... wasn't happy about that."

"I can see that!" the gray wyrm exclaims. Suddenly you start coughing, and you don't stop. You curl over as your body is wracked over and over. "Oh dear. Let's get you out of here, mortal. You need medical attention, badly." Your coughing eventually dies down, but you can't find the strength to muster any response. You curl up pitifully.

"I'll have to leg it. Hmm... you're in no state to hold yourself atop my back. I have an idea. Do NOT panic, understand?" You groan in response. Your stomach hurts so badly. Oh gods, what did Elizabeth do to you?

Nimbolus comes closer and leans his head to your level, jaws open. Wait. Is he really going to... ?

Then the damn dragon scoops you up into his jaws, and you freak out. He's eating you! He's going to kill you! You sacrificed everything for his damn species and he's paying you back by -

- you're not dead. Nimbolus isn't eating you. He's just keeping you at the base of his mouth with his giant, forked tongue over you to hold you in place. He jostles you around until you're facing outside his mouth. His fangs form a cage around you, but you can see the outside world. It smells, though. Like rotten meat and vomit. Do dragons have any concept of dental hygiene?

His plan dawns on you, but the smell combined with the disease magic Elizabeth's blasted you with makes your stomach lurch and you retch.

"Ach!" Nimbolus says around you. "Outside my mouth, outside!" he demands while pushing you with his tongue. You manage to grab onto his fangs with your armored hands and pull yourself up, hanging your head outside the sharp points of the dragon's teeth... and then you empty the contents of your stomach. It looks... distressingly red.

"Hang on!" Nimbolus mutters, before climbing down the Cathedral. He closes his teeth on you to keep you from falling, and once you're on the ground he opens his mouth back up and starts to run. Fresh air streams into his cavernous mouth as he plows through the Corinader swarms, away from the church and into the depths of Bovek. His gait is chaotic and wild, jostling you back and forth. You keep coughing. Your stomach hurts so much and everything's spinning.

As the world turns into nothing more than an ongoing wave of Corinaders surrounding the two of you, you pass out...
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Slowly, you open your eyes back up, groaning. Your stomach churns and your head throbs. You feel awful. Heavy and... vulnerable. Where are you? What happened?

You're in... an infirmary. You're in a bed, with white sheets covering your body. You're not wearing your armor. It's been replaced by leather clothing. There are plenty of other beds in the large wooden room, but all are empty. Many of them are pushed to the side, however, to make room for two dragons.

Lithoa and Corona are here, flat on their bellies with their wings tucked away to fit. "Ah, so he finally awakens!" Corona greets. "Welcome back, Kai. I suspect you are confused. Nimbolus came back to Mt. Vulcis in quite a hurry, demanding you be treated. It was a close thing. You were very, very injured, and there was an illness in your blood that would have killed you. You are better now, but it seems you will carry the scars of your battle for a while."

Lithoa nods. "Nimbolus gave us the details of your mission. You were to find the source of the Corinader repellent, correct? Did you find it?" she asks.

Weakly, you nod. "The Cathedral. Holy energy there, lots of it. And apparently the Corinaders are made of dark magic, so it repels them. Probably the same thing happening with other churches."

"I see," Corona says, nodding sagely. "Thank you very much. I'll be sure to deliver this information to Her Majesty. For now, however, I think it would be best if you rested here."

You nod, and rest your head in the pillows.

Your stomach churns nauseatingly and there are bandages around the burn wounds Elizabeth's lightning inflicted, but you're alive. Even if you feel as though a single shove could kill you.

Lithoa and Corona are both here, too. They're laying on their bellies with their wings tucked away to fit in the building. You wonder how they got in. Maybe the roof's retractable?
